Name
James Batke
Address
1333 W Parklane Blvd Apt 216
Chandler, Arizona. 85224
Phone Number
(805) 607-9315
Agenda Item No.
38
Support/Oppose
Oppose
Comments
I oppose the AI data center for the following reasons: 1. Energy Costs: There is wide reporting
about the high energy demand of AI data centers, with necessary infrastructure upgrades and
higher grid utilization expenses being passed on to local populations. I read an article with
comments from an attorney representing the developer, Adam Baugh, claiming that energy
concerns are overstated because they'll construct a power substation off-grid. Huh? My
understanding is that substations are connected to the grid and are used to basically right-size
the energy requirements for the connected facility, ergo citizens will still be on the hook for
increased consumption and infrastructural upgrades. Unless they're paying for their own
energy generation, which presents its own set of questions and concerns regarding the method
and environmental impacts entailed, then we are all going to be subsidizing their electric
cooling needs. 2. Energy Capacity and Emergency Concerns: I have grid capacity concerns
and doubts regarding whether we can rapidly upgrade infrastructure to account for the high
energy demands of AI data centers. There is an attraction to building these things in Arizona,
due to fewer natural disasters, but if AI energy usage results in sustained power outages, that
becomes a life or death concern in a desert climate. We should not risk this potential manmade
disaster. 3. Water Use: A closed-loop cooling system is better than an open-loop one, but my
understanding is that they do need to be frequently replenished and there are also indirect
water-use needs associated with the energy necessary to operate the closed-loop system, as
well as water requirements involved with the increased energy production necessary to operate
the entire facility. 4. Impact on Jobs: AI data centers do not offer many jobs beyond initial
construction, and we're talking about a technology explicitly marketed to c-suite executives as
a method to reduce labor overhead - discussing how many jobs an AI data center might create
is like discussing how many deck chairs there are on the Titanic. Why would Chandler
willingly act as a partner for a technology designed to kill jobs? Even the proposed Tech Park
office space seems like a gamble to be filled, and the 2 FTE condition per 1,000 square feet
does not promise a massive influx of jobs worth the trade off of having the AI data center
constructed. I agree with City Staff on this one - I would prefer the lot remain empty until a
more diversified set of high-skilled, sustainable jobs become an option for the area. 5. Global
Competitiveness: If the Federal government sees China's AI push as a threat, then they should
expend our tax dollars to construct new power stations separate from the civilian grid in
strategic areas not susceptible to resource scarcity (e.g. a desert), and we should have civilian
control of the AI data models. 7. The AI Bubble: We are facing a very uncertain and real risk
that the increased push for more computing power will not actually achieve the advancement
of AI technology sufficient enough to deliver upon its proposed promises. If or when this
realization occurs, investors will back out of their commitments and the need for AI data
centers will plummet precipitously. Why would Chandler take a gamble on a sunk cost for a
technology that may not even be viable? 6. Threat of Federal Preemption: I take an approach
to never concede in advance and expect my representatives to do the same. If the Feds bring a
fight to Chandler, I expect you to use every legal tool at your disposal to combat it. 7.
Electoral Considerations: The citizens of Chandler just graciously affirmed the democratic
right of a 2-term councilmember to run for mayor. I would suggest that any councilmember
with such ambitions should consider what a Yes vote on this agenda item might do for their
chances.

 Don't let this developer benefit from this project at the expense of our home values! Future impacts on our neighborhood if this project is approved include: Noise pollution: Data centers require massive cooling systems that run 24/7. This,

Don't let this developer benefit from this project at the expense of our home values! Future
impacts on our neighborhood if this project is approved include: Noise pollution: Data centers
require massive cooling systems that run 24/7. This, along with powerful backup diesel
generators, can create constant low-frequency humming that has been shown to cause
sleep disturbances, stress, and anxiety for nearby residents. Chandler's new data center
noise ordinance does not cover back-up generators. Strain on local resources: A single data
center can use as much water and electricity as a small town. This can lead to increased
costs for residents and strain local utility grids and water supplies, which is especially
---
1---
concerning in Chandler. Decreased property values: Constant noise, air pollution, and
obstruction of views will lower property values for homes near such facilities. The zoning
proposal requests buildings 90 feet tall which will block your views of our beautiful sunsets.
Air pollution: The large diesel generators used for backup power emit toxic air pollutants.
These are typically tested frequently, which will result in increased emissions. Limited local
job creation: Beyond the initial construction phase, data centers are highly automated and
create few long-term jobs for local residents. The Price Road Corridor has always been
Chandlers crown jewel for job employment; this use provides minimal jobs. Everyone must
contact our elected officials, say no to data centers and protect our property values and jobs!

Subject: Please vote NO on the proposed AI data center
Dear Members of the Chandler City Council, I’m writing as a concerned resident to respectfully urge you to vote no on the proposed AI data center. While I understand the desire to support economic development, this particular project raises
ZjQcmQRYFpfptBannerStart
This Message Is From an Untrusted Sender
You have not previously corresponded with this sender.
Report Suspicious
ZjQcmQRYFpfptBannerEnd
Dear Members of the Chandler City Council,
I’m writing as a concerned resident to respectfully urge you to vote no on the
proposed AI data center. While I understand the desire to support economic
development, this particular project raises serious long-term risks that I hope you
-----
---
1---
will take into full consideration before moving forward.
 
Cost & Resource Burden:
Data centers place enormous demands on local infrastructure, utilities, and public
services. The long-term financial cost to the city—both direct and indirect—will
far outweigh any short-term gains. Chandler residents should not be expected to
subsidize a project that consumes outsized resources.
Water Use & Potential Contamination:
In a region already facing water scarcity, the prospect of large-scale industrial
water consumption is deeply troubling. Beyond volume, there is legitimate
concern about contamination and the impact on groundwater quality. Once
damage is done, it is irreversible, and the burden falls entirely on the surrounding
communities.
Noise Pollution:
High-capacity cooling systems and constant mechanical operation produce
persistent noise, which will degrade the quality of life for nearby residents. Noise
pollution is not a minor nuisance—it has well-documented impacts on sleep,
stress levels, and overall health.
 
Grid Strain & Emergency Prioritization:
Data centers require massive, continuous electricity. As their demand grows, so
does the strain on our grid. I am concerned that, due to the financial relationship
between these corporations and utility providers, they may receive preferential
treatment during grid emergencies, leaving households and small businesses more
vulnerable to outages or reduced service.
 
Long-Term Health Impacts & Lack of Research:
There is still very limited research on the long-term health effects of living near
large-scale data centers, particularly regarding emissions, heat, and constant low-
frequency noise. Approving such a project without thorough, independent study
places surrounding communities into an uncontrolled experiment.
If the council chooses to move forward despite these risks, I strongly urge you to
require full transparency and ongoing public disclosure regarding the facility’s
usage of water, electricity, chemicals, and other resources. Chandler residents
deserve clarity, oversight, and access to real data—not assumptions or corporate
assurances.
 
Thank you for your time and for your commitment to protecting the well-being of
our community. I hope you will prioritize the long-term health, safety, and
sustainability of Chandler by voting no on this project.
Sincerely,
Amy Schwartz
Hearthstone Neighborhood

Hello I am writing my questions and concerns with regards to the proposal to put a AI data center
in Chandler county. I am purchasing a home and looking to move to Chandler right now because
of how beautiful and natural the area is, but I will seriously reconsider my move if the town is going
to allow a corporation to take their land and pollute it for profit. Putting a data center in Chandler
will cause a decrease in property value, destroy our beautiful natural desert, drive up electricity
costs, drain away our water, and causing health impacts from the pollution of our water and air.I
have some questions about the proposal. How are you weighing the costs that this will impose on
the community with regards to water consumption, electricity consumption, and environmental
pollution? What are the actual community benefits to having this data center in Chandler ?I believe
our water and electricity should go to people first and not to big AI hyper scale data centers that
dont provide any value to our communities and do more harm than good.The huge space that the
AI data center wants to use and the massive amount of electricity they will consume is ridiculous.
What do we get out of this? Nothing. Its not about what we get its about what they can take from
us. AI corporations are taking our land and our power from us. There is no reason they need to take
600 acres of land away for our community, that could go to improving our community and
preserving our natural environment, other than their own profit. There is no reason they need
enough electricity to power 1 million homes other than their own corporate greed. And that is just
what theyll take from us, what theyll leave us with is massive amounts of noise pollution, light
pollution, water pollution, dust pollution, and the destruction of our natural environment. Theres
examples of the destruction theyve done with other data centers in Tennessee and Virginia. The air
there is so polluted with dust and debris that it looks cloudy all the time, and the water is brown
and murky because these companies dont care about the rest of us or the environment and they
have shown they will just throw their trash in our land and pollute as much as they want.I dont
want our beautiful state of Arizona and Chandler to become that. I have too much pride in our
state for that. Please vote now and do not allow any land or water to go towards these AI data
centers. The land and water should be for the people first, dont allow them to take this from
us.Thank you,Cassandra Luna

ATTN: Chandler City Counsel Members
I am writing in opposition to the AI Data Center proposal being voted on this evening.
Forward progress often comes with hidden cost, but the cost of this data center is already
clear. 
We have been given this planet as a gift- a place to grow, breathe, and be human. As a
woman in my late 20s, I see the deficits and changes in the climate that began long before I
was here to care about the effects of human "progress" on our shared home. 
My generation has been forced to ask questions about the earth we have inherited:
1---
"Will there be enough water here in 3-5 years?"
"When will we it be our turn to face drought/famine/natural disaster?"
"Can we afford these programs and policies we are told are for our good, but are far too
often for someone else's gain leading to our loss?"
We have lost the art of asking "Should we?" instead of "Can we?". 
For the sake of this planet, the future home for our children and grandchildren, for our health,
our economy, and our creative minds, I am asking you to vote no against this creation of this
data center. At the heart, this is an endeavor against the people of Chandler and for greed,
lack of creativity, and harm to our home. 
As a concerned citizen who is trusting our elected leaders to do the right thing for us, we
urge you to vote NO this evening. 
Sincerely,
Charissa Peraud

Esteemed Mayor Hartke and Chandler City Council Members,
I am a lifelong Arizonan and have been a resident of the Chandler community for more than 40 years.
I truly grew up here. Every home I have owned has been in Chandler, and I have lived at my current
residence since 2000. When I moved into my home in Clemente Ranch, South Chandler looked quite
different. I could see mountains out west from my window and could still smell the dairy that once
stood at the corner of Dobson and Germann. Life in this beloved community has changed significantly
over the past 25 years—most changes for the better, and some, unfortunately, for the worse. The
proliferation of data centers along Dobson Road ranks among the most concerning of those negative
changes.
I am not naive; I understand that additional data centers will likely continue to be proposed and
1---
approved. I know enough about growth, perceived progress, and financial interests to recognize that
this trend will not simply stop. What I am asking—truly pleading—is that you use the authority
entrusted to you to prioritize the well-being of the community you represent, rather than the interests
of outside entities. There can be balance, and there can be outcomes that benefit all parties.
I participated in the community efforts years ago to request that effective attenuation measures were
added when data center buildings between Price and Dobson first started going up. Since my house
backs up to Dobson, I am on the front lines of this madness.  And, to the credit of both the City of
Chandler and CyrusOne, there were changes made to eliminate most of the noise.  I extend my
deepest gratitude to all parties who worked to reduce the noise pollution back in 2018-2019, as a few
of you on this current Council even met with us during that time.  If you decide to approve additional
data centers within our community, I ask that you at least require these companies to address and
mitigate the incessant, overwhelming noise that accompany them, preventing others from having to
deal with the hardship. 
Sadly, within the past 5-7 weeks, there has been a drastic resurgence of relentless noise coming from
across Dobson to once again, pollute our lives.  From what, I am not sure. I have reached out to the
company and am waiting for a response.  I am hoping this is an operational issue that they are quickly
working to resolve.  I would not wish the experience of living with this on anyone. I invite you to
spend just 20 minutes in my home or backyard to understand the impact this disruption has on one’s
peace, health, and sanity.  I have many audio recordings documenting the constant noise that residents
like me are now having to once again endure every minute of every day that I am happy to share.
Again, I am hoping that this is being resolved, but no further development details have been shared as
of yet to explain the unwelcomed return of the noise. 
The decisions of some past Councils have been misguided, and I would say even negligent to the
community it attempted to serve.  I implore you not to revisit that pattern. Please remember that, as
elected officials, your duty is to serve as stewards of our people, our resources, and our city. The
choices you make matter deeply. I ask that you make them matter for the good of all in this
community.
Kindly,
Ms. Cheryl Jannuzzi

Dear Mayor and Members of the Chandler City Council,
My name is Christi Misener and I live Talante at the corner of Alma School and Germannin Chandler.
I am writi ng to ask you to oppose the proposed AI data center at Price & Dobson, less than 1,000
feet from existing homes.
This project would fundamentally change our neighborhood. I am concerned about issues such as
------
---
1---
24/7 industrial noise, heavy truck traffic, water and power demands, air quality, and the risk of large
battery systems so close to families. This site is surrounded by homes, schools, and parks — it is not
an appropriate location for a data-center complex.
Please protect existing Chandler neighborhoods and keep industrial-scale data farms away from
residential areas. I urge you to vote NO on any zoning or approvals that would allow the Price Road
data center to move forward at this location, and to direct staff to look for more suitable sites farther
from homes.
Thank you for your service to our city and for listening to residents like me.
Sincerely,
Christi Misener

Agenda Item No.
Resolution 5957
Support/Oppose
Oppose
Comments
Dear Chandler City Council, I strongly urge you to reject Resolution 5957 and the
development agreement for an AI data center at 3380 S. Price Road. This project will place a
heavy, long-term burden on our community by draining scarce water resources, driving up
electrical demand and straining infrastructure in a region where we already face heat, drought
and rising costs of living. The limited number of jobs it creates does not justify these risks.
Chandler should focus on growth that supports residents -- not on projects that benefit a
limited few while compromising our water, environment and future livability. Please reject.
Thank you for your consideration.

Dear Mayor, Vice Mayor, and Council Members, It looks like it's all but a done deal, but I am writing to urge you to vote against approving this project for two primary reasons--our precious water resources and the demand it will pull from
ZjQcmQRYFpfptBannerStart
This Message Is From an Untrusted Sender
You have not previously corresponded with this sender.
Report Suspicious
ZjQcmQRYFpfptBannerEnd
Dear Mayor, Vice Mayor, and Council Members,
- --
---
1---
It looks like it's all but a done deal, but I am writing to urge you to vote against approving this
project for two primary reasons--our precious water resources and the demand it will pull
from the valley's power grid.
I have read through the proposal and completed hours of investigation online including
reading City of Chandler's webpage on water conservation and drought-preparedness
(https://www.chandleraz.gov/residents/water/water-conservation/drought-preparedness).  I
have read where the site will have a closed loop water system using reclaimed water, but I
remain convinced that the completion and use of
 this project as a data center will have long-
term adverse effects on Chandler, the metropolitan area and the state.  The CAP provides
approximately 37% of the city's water and our allotment will be cut each time it is up for
review because the Colorado River is suffering from continued depletion.  While the future is
uncertain, it is dangerous to assume this is going to improve.  Although Chandler may have
20-30 years in reserves stored underground, we keep growing which means the amount of
total water used each year will continue to grow even in light of the conservation measures
and reclaimed water being utilized.  While you and I may be dead and gone before we see
water at an absolute crisis level, it is only a matter of time.  The decision to locate another
data center in the desert is the council's legacy.
In addition to the literal drain on our water supply, data centers are a sizable risk to our
power grid.  You can ask anyone who is located near one in other places.  They will all tell
you their bills have increased dramatically and their reliable supply of power when needed
most has been disrupted.  I understand there are staff at SRP and APS who are quietly
concerned about how they are going to continue to meet demand going forward.  I have
been a valley resident since 1982 and a Chandler resident since 1990, I remember the days
of short-lived brown-outs in the heat of the day when we couldn't get air conditioning for a
couple of hours.  I do not want to return to those times.
The Phoenix metro area already has 154 data centers in operation-twelve of which are in
Chandler- near as I can tell.  They bring very little employment to our citizens and consume
more power and water per square foot than any household or any other type of business by
a large margin.  I urge you to consider the long-term consequences of this choice and
disapprove of this data center. And I urge you to consider the following questions:
1. Is there any coordination of planning efforts between valley cities to make sure we
don't have too many data centers draining our resources?  This and other data centers
have the potential to impact citizens far beyond each city's borders.
2.  What percentage of actual Chandler water rights will be reduced with the reductions in
water from the Colorado water reduction agreement in 2026 and beyond? 
3. How will the potential joining of the Salt and Verde River systems with the Colorado
River affect Chandler's allotment?
4.  What percentage of our water allotment are we currently using?
5.  What criteria is used by Chandler to establish "best and highest use" for our precious
water? Is the city discussing how those criteria may need to change in the future?
6.  What is the benefit to Chandler and its citizens from this and other data centers?
Direct money payments to the city, temporary and permanent jobs, etc.?  I don't really
-- -- -------
see a strong upside to this type of development.
7. Does Chandler require an environmental impact study for data centers?  If so, who
conducts the study?  Water/power usage, noise pollution, air pollution from power
generating sources?  I could not find any information that indicated environmental
studies were completed.
8. What is the purpose of approving the Mid-Rise Overlay which appears to be 7 or 8
stories high?  Have the impacts of this been considered to residents in the adjacent
neighborhoods?
9. Why are we entertaining the operation of a data center by a foreign LLC?  Yes, it's
legal, but is it in Chandler residents' best interest?
Thank you for your time and attention.
Julie Grahmann

37
Comments
Whatever company wants to build this building for manufacturing chips should be responsible, if
the condition arises that they use an extraordinary amount of electricity and water, that the
company be responsible for paying to bring the additional electricity in. For instance, if SRP says
they need to build more facilities for producing electricity, the company wanting to build the chip
building should be responsible for paying for additional SRP facilities. The residents of Chandler
should not have their electric bills raised due to SRP having to build any additional facilities. The
same holds true for water. If this chip building consumes an inordinate amount of water, Chandler
residents should not have to pay for any additional water facilities. The chip building could dig their
own well, or pay the city of Chandler to enlarge the water facilities. Also, they should pay the usual
amount of city taxes that any other company would pay; they should not be given any special tax
discounts.

that caused a global economic crisis.Its bad for our environment. The energy and water needs

asthma deaths per year by 2030 in the US alone. This proposed project will be bad for the City

vote for another potential economic crisis, for utility rate hikes, for the continued destruction
of our education system, to jobs, and to the deaths of community members surrounding this
project. Thank you for your time!

Mayor Hartke and Chandler City Council Members-
I am a Chandler resident and am reaching out to express my opposition to the City Council’s
consideration of the Price Road Data Center.
I have lived in this area for the past 28 years; my wife and I have raised our two boys in the area and
have long time roots in the community. Both our boys attended Chandler Unified School District
schools through high school. We chose this area as the ideal community with a family feel and lots of
future potential. 
-------
1---
However, it is concerning that our City Council is considering allowing this high-density computing
center within 1,000 feet of my fellow neighbors. Chandler was built with a community feel, embracing
the history of the small farm town it once was. The consideration of this development is
counterintuitive to all that the City of Chandler represents.  
In addition, the noise produced from the industrial complex; the regular testing of backup generators,
increase in industrial traffic and amount of power that will be utilized will be crippling to our
community.
Our state is currently facing a water drought and yet at the same time the Council is considering such
a development that will further negatively impact our access to that vital and yet limited resource.
Finally, as a Chandler homeowner any consideration of a development that could have a negative
impact on our property value is of the upmost concern.
It is with these concerns; I am asking the Chandler City Council and Mayor Hartke to deny the zoning
request for the Proposed Price Road Data Center.
Thank you for your consideration. 
Rich Groppenbacher
602-321-6444

612 W Flintlock Way

Agenda Item No.
37 and 38
Support/Oppose
Oppose
Comments
Good evening Chair and Commissioners. My name is Steven Ruf, and I'm speaking in
opposition to the rezoning at 3380 South Price Road. Chandlers General Plan is a voter-
ratified vision, and it is very clear about what the South Price Road Corridor is intended to be.
The Plan describes this area as one of Chandlers premier employment corridors and
specifically calls for development that supports high-quality jobs, knowledge-based industries,
and single-user, campus-style environments. It also states that rezonings must maintain
consistency with the Future Land Use Map and established employment objectives.Your own
planning staff determined that this proposal does not meet those policies. A data center a low-
employment, high-infrastructure use does not provide the high-value job density the General
Plan requires in this corridor.Beyond policy misalignment, there are still serious
environmental and health concerns that have not been fully addressed.Data centers bring
continuous mechanical noise, and the Plan explicitly emphasizes protecting community well-
being through mitigation of noise, vibration, and other environmental impacts on surrounding
development. Backup diesel generators contribute particulate emissions, yet we have no
comprehensive air-quality analysis. And water use remains a major unknown a critical issue in
a city where the Plan highlights the need to promote long-term water stewardship and
sustainability in all development decisions.This project also undermines the Plans broader
environmental goals. Chandler commits to reducing heat exposure and improving
environmental health, yet data centers generate significant waste heat and require substantial
energy infrastructure that was never envisioned for this corridor.Finally, the promised
employment buildings are still speculative. As CitizenPortal reported, the data center is the
only fully defined phase, while the job-producing components lack confirmed tenants and firm
commitments. The General Plan cautions against exactly this scenario, emphasizing that
development should advance economic goals, not merely entitlements.Approving this
rezoning now would contradict the General Plan, ignore staffs recommendation, and lock in a
use with unresolved environmental and resource impacts. For these reasons, I respectfully ask
you to uphold the General Plans policies and deny this rezoning request. Thank you.

Agenda Item No.
Resolution No. 5957
Support/Oppose
Oppose
Comments
I urge the Council to reject Resolution No. 5957 because it advances without the transparency,
data, or resident engagement Chandler deserves. There is no clear analysis showing how this
proposal will mitigate increased traffic congestion, protect neighborhood safety, or manage rising
infrastructure and utility strain especially as Chandlers population has grown by more than 15%
over the past decade. The resolution also lacks clarity on long-term fiscal impact, potential
environmental effects, and how it aligns with current zoning plans designed to preserve community
character. Approving a measure without publicly available impact data puts our neighborhoods at
risk and undermines the evidence driven decision-making our city is known for. I strongly ask the
Council to reject this resolution and require a transparent, data-backed process centered on the
people who live here.

Honorable Mayor and City Council, I am writing to express my strong support for the proposed Price Road Innovation Campus, which would redevelop the former Orbital Sciences site into a modern technology and innovation hub, including a data center. 
ZjQcmQRYFpfptBannerStart
This Message Is From an Untrusted Sender

Honorable Mayor and City Council,
I am writing to express my strong support for the proposed Price Road Innovation Campus,
which would redevelop the former Orbital Sciences site into a modern technology and
innovation hub, including a data center. I am a South Chandler resident (85286), and I
commute several days per week to the West Valley where I worked in the AI Data center
space at Microsoft Prior to this, I worked for Intel Ocotillo in industrial water reuse. Through
this industry experience, I understand the rigorous efficiency and sustainability standards
modern data centers must meet and the critical role they play in strengthening digital
resilience for residents, schools, small- and large-scale businesses, government, defence
and ongoing technological advancement.
1---
As I drive along the Price Road Corridor many days each week, I have observed several
notable vacant or underutilized employment sites—including parcels long zoned for major
employment uses. Thoughtful (re)development of these parcels, especially those that have
remained unoccupied for many years, would continue to benefit South Chandler and the
residents who have chosen to live here because of the community’s strong commitment to
innovation and corresponding high-laying employment opportunities.
It is well known that Chandler has long been at the forefront of advanced technology and
manufacturing. However, while Chandler is already home to data center employers such as
CyrusOne and Align, our presence in this booming critical sector lags behind cities like Mesa
and Goodyear, which have successfully attracted leading companies including AWS, Google,
Apple, Meta, Microsoft, and others, subsequently creating significant opportunities for tech
collocation, multi-million dollar investment, and job growth.
By supporting AI-focused data center development, the City has an opportunity to lead the
way in this expanding and critical industry. For these reasons, I support the proposed project
and respectfully urge the City Council to approve the proposed rezoning and allow the Price
Road Innovation Campus to move forward.
Thank you for your time and consideration,
John Bombardier
Chandler Resident
Sr. Engineer Microsoft | Ex-Intel

Support
Comments
I write in support of any pending council action on rezoning or approving construction of data
centers in Chandler. I am unable to attend in person in the upcoming meeting, but the irony is
not lost on this Chandler resident that an online forum is available to formally lodge my
comment. Indeed I only became aware of this debate through an online forum. The concerns
of the opponents of this data center are not invalid, however a vocal minority should not be the
final word for a silent majority who welcomes necessary infrastructure to keep Chandler and
the Valley overall on the cutting edge of business and technological development. Those who
would oppose have concerns about power and water consumption. Valid concerns; and
Council should be aware of working with other local and regional stakeholders in developing
broader expansion of power and water production as the economy of the Valley continues to
expand. However, using the Council as a veto is parochial and short-sighted. I speak as a
resident, with no financial stake or particular interest in whether this project gets approved or
not. But I speak to Chandler's history of welcoming new technological development; from
Motorola and Intel historically, to the changing landscape of new firms who will make our
increasingly data-driven modern lives robust and accessible. The opposition is vocal, and the
support likely motivated by self-interest. But at the end, there are those of us who are happy
with progress, and I post this comment only to reaffirm that progress is welcome, even if we
aren't there in person with picket signs to reaffirm it.
